Among his other exegetical works were commentaries on the Gospel and Revelation of St. John, the Minor Prophets, Ecclesiastes, and especially the Canticles and Matthew. In these works he follows the text conscientiously and laboriously, verse by verse. The Canticles Rupert regarded as a song in honor of the Virgin Mary, but he set himself against the doctrine that she was conceived without sin. The commentary opens with an interpretation of Cant. 1:2, thus: " ’Let him kiss me with the kiss of his mouth.’ What is this exclamation so great, so sudden? Of blessed Mary, the inundation of joy, the force of love, the torrent of pleasure have filled thee full and wholly intoxicated thee and thou hast felt what eye has not seen nor ear heard nor has entered into the heart of man, and thou hast said, ’Let him kiss me with the kiss of his mouth’ for thou didst say to the angel ’Behold the handmaid of the Lord, let it be unto me according to thy word.’ What was that word? What did he say to thee? ’Thou hast found grace,’ he said, ’with the Lord. Behold thou shalt conceive and bare a son.’... Was not this the word of the angel, the word and promise of the kiss of the Lord’s mouth ready to be given?" etc.(156)

Rupert also has a place in the history of the doctrine of the Lord’s Supper, and it is an open question whether or not he substituted the doctrine of impanation for the doctrine of transubstantiation.(157)

Original Footnotes

(1; original 1312): 1. Milman, Hist. of Lat. Christianity, VIII. 257, is certainly unjust when he says: "With all their search into the unfathomable, the Schoolmen have fathomed nothing; with all their vast logical apparatus, they have proved nothing to the satisfaction of the inquisitive mind." One has only to think of the ontological argument of Anselm and the cosmological arguments of Thomas Aquinas and the statements wrought out on the satisfaction of Christ to feel that the statement is not true.

(2; original 1313): See Roger Bacon: Opus Majus, Bridges’ ed. I. 54-56; Sandys, Class. Scholarship, pp. 507, 540-546, 568-sqq., and Seth, Enc. Brit., XXI. 419.

(3; original 1314): The council of Paris, 1209, forbade the use of his Natural Philosophy. Gregory IX., 1231, condemned the Physics, but in 1254 the University of Paris prescribed the number of hours to be devoted to the explanation of Aristotle’s works.

(4; original 1315): Omnis hic excluditur, omnis est abjectus.

Qui non Aristotelis venit armis tectus. Chart., I. p. xviii.

(5; original 1316): Cousin made three periods, the first when philosophy was in subjection to theology, the second when they were in union, and the third when they were separated.

(6; original 1317): "Otherness," applied by Rich. de St. Victor to the Trinitarian distinctions.

(7; original 1318): de sacram., I. 7; Migne’s ed., 176, 290.

(8; original 1319): Peltier’s ed., V. 38.

(9; original 1320): Thomas Fuller quaintly compared the Schoolmen to those who built their houses in London on small patches of ground "improving their small bottom with towering speculations."

(10; original 1321): H. Doergens, Lehre von d. Universalien, Heidelb., 1867; J. H. Löwe, D. Kampf zwischen d. Realismus und Nominalismus im Mittelalter, Prag, 1876. Art."Universalien," in Wetzer-Welte, XII. 305 sqq. The Histt. of Philosophy.

(11; original 1322): According to John of Salisbury there were no less than thirteen different shades of opinion on the subject. See Prantl, Gesch. der Logik, II. 118.

(12; original 1323): The passage from Porphyry runs—mox de generibus et speciebus illud quidem sive subsistant, sive in solis nudis intellectibus posita sint, sive subsistentia corporalia sint an incorporalia, et utrum separata a sensibilibus, an insensibilibus posita et circa haec consistentia, dicere recusabo. Altissimum enim negotium est hujusmodi et majoris egens inquisitionis. See Gieseler, Ch. Hist., Germ. ed., III. 384.

(13; original 1324): Otto of Freising, de gest. Frid., I. 47, spoke of him as the originator of Nominalism in that age, qui primus nostris temporibus in logica sententiam vocum instituit. According to John of Salisbury, nominalism almost wholly vanished with Roscellinus, Metalog., II. 17.

(14; original 1325): Pseudo-Dialecticus. Ep., 21. De fide trin. 3. tres personae sunt tres res sicut tres angeli aut tres animae, ita tamen ut voluntas et potestas omnino sunt idem. Also Ep., II. 41.

(15; original 1326): Loofs, p. 271, says, "He is perhaps the most important of all the mediaeval theologians."

(16; original 1327): Church gives a graphic picture of "wild Aosta lulled by Alpine rills." Aosta was a Roman settlement bearing the name Augusta Praetoria, and was made a bishopric about the fifth century.

(17; original 1328): His views were set forth in the de processione Spiritus Sancti. He argued that the Spirit proceeded from the Father not as father but as God. He must therefore also proceed from the Son as God.

(18; original 1329): See quotations in Freeman, W. Rufus, II. 661.

(19; original 1330): Paradiso, XII. 137.

(20; original 1331): Freeman has an excursus on Anselm’s letters in his W. Rufus, II. 570-588.

(21; original 1332): Qui non crediderit, non experietur, et qui expertus non fuerit non intelliget, de fide trin., 2; Migne, 158, 264.

(22; original 1333): Ep., II. 41;Migne, 158. 1193, Christianus per fidem debet ad intellectum proficere non per intellectum ad fidem.

(23; original 1334): Cur Deus homo, I. 2; Migne, 158. 364.

(24; original 1335): Eadmer: nihil asserere nisi quod aut canonicis aut Augustini dictis posse defendi videret.

(25; original 1336): aliquid quo majus nihil cogitari potest.

(26; original 1337): si vel in solo intellectu est, potest cogitari esse et in re, quod majus est.

(27; original 1338): Thomas Aquinas said that, even if the name of God means illud quo majus cogitari non potest, yet it would not be possible to proceed to the affirmation of God’s real existence, because the atheist denies that there is aliquid quo majus cogitari non potest, Summa, I. ii. 2. Hegel replied to Kant that the Begriff an und für sich selbst enthält das Sein also eine Bestimmtheit. Professor E. Caird, in an article, Anselm’s Argument for the Being of God (Journal of Theolog. Studies, 1900, pp. 23-39), sums up his objection to Anselm’s argument by saying, "It is the scholastic distortion of an idea which was first presented in the Platonic philosophy," etc. Ritschl, Justification and Reconciliation, p. 217, makes the same objection when he says Anselm confuses reality and thought.

(28; original 1339): intelligere andin intellectu esse.

(29; original 1340): A careful statement of the history of the ontological argument was given by Köstlin, D. Beweise fürs Dasein Gottes, in Studien u. Kritiken, 1875, 1876. Also Ruze, D. ontol. Gottesbeweis seit Anselm, Halle, 1882.

(30; original 1341): Quamvis homo juste a diabolo torqueretur, ipse tamen illum injuste torquebat, etc., I. 7; Migne, 158. 367 sq. Again Anselm takes up this point, II. 20; p. 427 sq., and says it was not necessary for God to descend to conquer the devil or to proceed judicially against him in order to liberate man. Nothing else did God owe the devil but punishment, and nothing else did man owe the devil but to treat him as he had been treated, that is, to conquer him as man himself had been conquered. All that was demanded by the devil, man owed to God and not to the devil.

(31; original 1342): Non aliud est peccare quam Deo non reddere debitum. I. 11; Migne, p. 376.

(32; original 1343): pro contumelia illata plus reddere quam abstulit .... Debet omnis qui peccat, honorem quem rapuit, Deo solvere et haec est satisfactio quam omnis peccator Deo debet facere.

(33; original 1344): Satisfactio quam nec potest facere nisi Deus nec debet nisi homo, necesse est ut eam faciat Deus-homo, II. 6; Migne, p. 404.

(34; original 1345): II. 22; Migne, 158. 431. It is a matter of dispute how far Anselm drew upon the doctrine of penance which had been handed down from the Fathers or from the German law with its Wehrgeld, or debt of honor; or whether he drew upon them at all. It is probable that the Church’s penitential system had affected the chivalric idea of honor. Harnack, Dogmengesch., III. 252 sq., and Ritschl, Justification, etc., p. 263, make the objection against Anselm’s argument that it was based upon an "idea of God’s justice which implies an equality in private rights between God and man."

(35; original 1346): Harnack gives prolonged attention to Anselm’s argument (Dogmengesch., III. 341-358) and, in specifying its merits and defects, declares that the defects largely outweigh the merits. Anselm’s theory is not at all to be adopted, die Theorie ist völlig unannehmbar. It would not be necessary, Harnack says, to waste many words over the defects if it were not that the theology of the present day is stuck in traditionalism and neglects all the canons of Gospel, ethics, logic, and culture. He declares it to be a fearful thought that God may not forgive from pure love, but had to have his honor appeased by sacrfice. Anselm’s argument taken by itself does not justify such severe criticism, and, if his other writings and his own character be taken into account, he will be absolved from the implied charges.

(36; original 1347): Meditationes seu Orationes, Migne, 158. 709-1014. See Hasse, I. 176-232.

(37; original 1348): Jesu bone, quam dulcis es in corde cogitantis de te et diligentis te, Migne, 158. 770.

(38; original 1349): Rule, I. 48, describes from personal observation the ancient and dizzy bridge, le Pont de l’Aël, over a torrent near Aosta, which, as he says, Anselm in making his description may have had in mind.

(39; original 1350): Sine mensura profundum, et tenebrosa caligine horribiliter obscurum, Migne, 158, 719.

(40; original 1351): The later Schoolmen did not lean back upon Anselm’s theology as we might have expected them to do. He was, however, often quoted, as by Thomas Aquinas and Albertus Magnus, e.g., Summa, I. 3, 13, etc., Borgnet’s ed., XXXI. 60, 69, 326.

(41; original 1352): From this point and the enmity of William, he dates his misfortunes. Hinc calamitatum mearum quae nunc perseverant coeperunt exordia et quo amplius fama extendebatur nostra, aliena in me succensa est invidia, Migne, p. 116.

(42; original 1353): Verborum usum habebat mirabilem, sed sensu contemptibilem et ratione vacuum, Migne, p. 123.

(43; original 1354): Ep., 326; St. Bernard’s Works, Migne, 182. 531.

(44; original 1355): Remusat gives an attractive picture of his appearance, I. 43 sq.

(45; original 1356): Ep., II.; Migne, 178, 188.

(46; original 1357): See his description, I. 47 sqq.

(47; original 1358): A letter is preserved written by Abaelard to his son. It indicates affection. The father urges him to study the Scriptures. An Astralabius is mentioned as belonging to the chapter of Nantes in 1150. Hausrath, p. 173, conjectures he was Abaelard’s son.

(48; original 1359): Ut amplius mitagarem, obtuli me ei satisfacere eam scilicet quam corruperam mihi matrimonio copulando, dummodo id secreto fieret, ne famae detrimentum incurrerem. Migne, p. 130.

(49; original 1360): Concupiscentia te mihi potius quam amicitia sociavit, libidinis ardor potius quam amor. Ep., II.; Migne, p. 186.

(50; original 1361): Deutsch, p. 35. So war Abaelard Mönch geworden, nicht von innerem Verlangen getrieben, etc. His relations with Heloise made freedom in his position as a public teacher in the open for the time impossible.

(51; original 1362): Introductio in theologiam. Abaelard is our chief authority for the trial. Hist. Calam., Migne, pp. 141-150. See Otto of Freising.

(52; original 1363): Abaelard closes his autobiography by declaring that like another Cain he was dragged about the earth, a fugitive and vagabond, but also by quoting passages upon the providence of God as that all things work together for good to them that love Him.

(53; original 1364): Ep. Bernardi, 326; Migne, 132. 531 sqq. William sent to Bernard Abaelard’s Theologia and other works to make good his charges. He feared Abaelard would become "a dragon" whom no one could destroy. Kutter, in his Wilhelm von St. Thierry, pp. 34, 36, 43, 48, insists, as against Deutsch, that William was the exciting originator of the trial of Abaelard, which was soon to follow, and that Bernard preferred silence and peace to conflict, and was amused to action by William’s appeal.

(54; original 1365): Ep. Abael., X.; Migne, 178. 335.

(55; original 1366): Bernard’s biographer, Gaufrid, states that Abaelard promised amendment. No reference was made to such a promise in the charges at Sens, an omission difficult to understand if the promise was really made. See Remusat, I. 172, and Poole, p. 163.

(56; original 1367): Ep. Bernardi, 189; Migne, 182. 355. Bernard describes the meeting and sets forth the danger from Abaelard’s influence, Epp. 187-194, 330-338. For an account of this trial, see my art., "St. Bernard the Churchman" in Princeton Rev., 1903, pp. 180 sqq.

(57; original 1368): This preliminary meeting rests upon the testimony of Berengar and upon a passage in John of Salisbury, Hist. Pontif., chap. VIII. 9. John, in describing the trial of Gilbert of Poictiers, says Bernard wanted to have Gilbert’s case prejudged in a preliminary sitting and by the same method he had resorted to in the case of Abaelard, —arte sim ili magistrum Petrum agressus erat. Berengar’s defence of Abaelard descends to passionate invective. Migne, 178. 1858 sqq. Berengar represents the bishops and Bernard as being heated with wine at this preliminary conference, when they decided against Abaelard. The details of his account and his charges against Bernard are altogether out of accord with his character as it is otherwise known to us. Deutsch (Neander’s St. Bernard, II. 1 sqq.) cannot free Bernard from unfairness in the part he took at this conference, as Vacandard does.

(58; original 1369): The statement is not inconsistent with the representation of Otto of Freising, a disinterested reporter, who gives as reason for refusing to make an argument that he feared a popular tumult.

(59; original 1370): Migne, 182. 1049-1051. Also Hefele, V. 463 sqq.

(60; original 1371): Ep., 331; Migne, 182. 537. There are nine of these letters to the cardinals, 188, 192, 193, 331-335, 338. The longest letter was the one addressed to the pope, 190; Migne, 182. 1051-1071. The great vehemence of these letters have exposed Bernard in some quarters to unmitigated condemnation. From the standpoint of Christian moderation and charity they are difficult to understand and cannot be justified. Hausrath, p. 248, etc., represents him as der werltkluge Abt von Clairvaux, resorting to all the arts of diplomacy to secure a verdict against Abaelard. M’Cabe, in a very readable chapter, pp. 322-354, takes the same view. Without excusing him, it must be remembered in passing judgment that heresy was regarded with horror in that age. Bernard, no doubt, also shrank from Abaelard as a man who sought applause rather than the advancement of the Church. Morison, p. 302, speaks "of a horror of great darkness falling upon Bernard," when he recognized the dangers of a new era. Neander, St. Bernard, II. 3, says that no one can question that Bernard’s zeal proceeded from a pure Christian purpose, but that he used the weapons of hatred under the mask of holy love.

(61; original 1372): Migne, 178. 103.

(62; original 1373): The Story of Misfortunes was written while he was abbot of St. Gildas. It has been compared to the Confessions of Augustine. But no comparison could more sadly offend against truth. Abaelard revealed his inward states to gain a worldly end. He wanted to draw attention to himself and prepare the way for a new career. His letters to Heloise are not so much to assure her of his orthodoxy as to make that impression upon the Church authorities. This is the position taken by Deutsch, pp. 43 sqq., Hausrath, 275 sqq., and Nitsch, art. Abaelard in Herzog.

(63; original 1374): The French writers designate Abaelard’s theory Conceptualism, and hold that he substituted conceptus for voces. Deutsch, p. 105. Walter Map, writing in the second half of the twelfth century, speaks of Abaelard as "the leader of the Nominalists, princeps nominalismi, who sinned more in dialectics than he did in his treatment of Scripture." Wright’s ed., I. 24, p. 41.

(64; original 1375): See also Introd. ad Theol., Migne, 178. 980.

(65; original 1376): Dubitando ad inquisitionem venimus, inquirendo veritatem percipimus. Sic et Non, Migne, p. 1349. Deutsch, pp. 159 sq., speaks of this spirit of free inquiry, Die Freiheit der Forschung, as the note running through all Abaelard’s writings.

(66; original 1377): Hist. Calam., Migne, 178. 142. Nec credi posse aliquid nisi primitus intellectus, etc.

(67; original 1378): Introd. ad Theol., Migne, p. 1051, also p. 959. Fides quippe dicitur existimatio non apparentium, cognitio vero ipsarum rerum experientia per ipsam earam praesentiam.

(68; original 1379): Credimus ut cognoscamus; nisi credideritis, non intelligetis. See other quotations in Hefele, V. 463-469; also Deutsch, in his chapter on Faith and Knowledge, pp. 168 sqq.

(69; original 1380): So the charges of Bernard and the Synod of Sens, and Otto of Freising. De gestis Frid., 48.

(70; original 1381): Introd. ad Theol., Migne, p. 986.

(71; original 1382): Introd. ad Theol., Migne, p. 1052.

(72; original 1383): Catholica quippe est fides, id est universalis quae ita omnibus necessaria est ut nemo discretus absque ea salvari possit, Migne, p. 986. In view of such a statement, Poole’s remark has much in its favor, "it was not really Abaelard’s results that formed the strength of the indictment against him, but the method by which he reached them," p. 153.

(73; original 1384): They are found in his Com. on Romans, as well as in his Introd. ad Theol. and his Sermons, V., X., XII.

(74; original 1385): They are set forth more particularly in the ethical treatise Scito te ipsum and the Com. on Romans, especially in an excursus on original sin, appended to chap. V., Migne, pp. 866-874.

(75; original 1386): He thinks the tree whose fruit excited the sexual passions was the vine. Hexameron, Migne, p. 777.

(76; original 1387): Com. on Romans, chap. II. 6. Deutsch, pp. 344 sqq., deals at length with Abaelard’s views on Sin.

(77; original 1388): Introd. ad Theol., Migne, p. 1008.

(78; original 1389): Hausrath, pp. 293 sqq., assigns to Abaelard a place in the front rank of such martyrs. He justifies him for declining to stand by his conclusions in these words: "It would be unfair to demand that a scholar, who was under the pressure of such circumstances (that is mediaeval ecclesiasticism should have the courage of a farm hand, or carry his views to their logical conclusion like a statesman."

(79; original 1390): Abaelard left admiring pupils, some of whom, like Omnibene, wrote books of Sentences based upon their teacher’s Theology, and followed his threefold division of faith, the Sacraments, and love. See Denifle, Archiv, pp, 613 sqq.

(80; original 1391): Denifle includes the Lombard in the theological school of Abaelard. See his Abaelard’s Sentenzen und d. Bearbeitungen seiner Theologie, Archiv, 1885, pp. 613-624.

(81; original 1392): Neander-Deutsch, St. Bernard, II. 131. Poole, p. 181, calls Gilbert’s exposition of the Trinity "one of the subtlest and most elaborate contributions to theological metaphysics the Middle Ages produced."

(82; original 1393): Hist. pontif., VIII.; Migne, pp. 522 sqq. One of the accusers was Adam du Petit Pont, an Englishman, afterwards bishop of St. Asaph. He got his name from the school he set up on a little bridge connecting Paris with the Latin quarter. Schaarschmidt, p. 13.

(83; original 1394): Otto of Freising states the four detailed charges as follows: 1. divina essentia non est deus. 2. proprietates personarum non sunt ipsae personae. 3. theolog. personae in nulla praedicantur propositione. 4. dimna natura non est incarnata. Gaufried, Migne, 185. 617, states the first three a little differently.

(84; original 1395): Stephens calls him "by far the most distinguished English scholar of his century."Hist. of the Engl. Ch., pp. 320 sqq.

(85; original 1396): Schaarschmidt calls it "the first great theory of the state in the literature of the Middle Ages." In view of the variety of its contents, Poole, p. 218, says that "it is to some extent an encyclopaedia of the cultivated thought of the middle of the twelfth century."

(86; original 1397): Poole says, "No writer of his age can be placed beside him in the extent and depth of his classical reading."Dict. of Natl. Biog., XXIX. 441. Schaarschmidt speaks of his marvellous acquaintance with the classics—eine staunenswerthe Vertrautheit.

(87; original 1398): Metalog., VII. 2.

(88; original 1399): This is the date given on an ancient epitaph in Paris, but the date is made uncertain by the appointment of a bishop of Paris as the Lombard’s successor, 1160. This would seem to indicate his death occurred at that time unless he was deposed on the charge of simony, of which, as Walter of St. Victor says, he was guilty. Migne, 199. 1140.

(89; original 1400): Liber quem dicunt sententarium, Ep., 188; Migne, 182. 668. Walter of St. Victor declares it to have been by Abaelard’s hand or taken from his works, aut ex libris ejus excerptus. See Deutsch, P. Abaelard excursus.

(90; original 1401): Denifle, Archiv, 1885, learnedly establishes the relation of these works to Abaelard. They exist in MSS. at Nürnberg, Munich, etc. Omnebene expressly declared his work to be a compilatlon taken from different sources.

(91; original 1402): Sententiarum theologicarum libri, VII.; Migne, vol. 186. His name is spelt Pullein, Pullan, etc. See Rashdall’s art. in Dict. of Nat’l Biogr., XLVII. 19.

(92; original 1403): The Jesuit Possevin gives a list of 246 commentaries in print. See Wetzer-Welte, IX. 1921, which speaks of the number of commentaries as unzaelig, "without number." Hergenröther (Gesch. II. 516) speaks of them in the same way as zahllos. The first commentary, according to Werner (Thom. von Aquino, I. 314), was by William of Seignelay, teacher in Paris and later bishop of Paris.

(93; original 1404): Prolog. to the Sentences, brevi volumine complicans patrum sententias appositis eorum eorum testimoniis, etc.

(94; original 1405): Baltzer, pp. 2-5, gives the results of a careful study. Augustine furnishes 1000 quotations. Hilary comes next, being quoted 86 times. Baltzer’s book is a laborious comparison of every paragraph of the Lombard with the Fathers and his predecessors among the Schoolmen, especially Abaelard and Hugo of St. Victor.

(95; original 1406): Denifle (Archiv, pp. 621 sqq.) is authority for the statement that he also quotes from Gandulf’s Sentences which still remain in MS. at Turin.

(96; original 1407): Migne, 192. 522.

(97; original 1408): Reprobatio Dei est praescientia militiae in quibusdam non finiendae, et praeparatio poenae non terminandae.

(98; original 1409): II. 31; Migne, p. 211.

(99; original 1410): Mors nos justificat, dum per eam caritas excitatur in cordibus nostris, III. 19; Migne, p. 285. John of Cornwall, his pupil, expressly says that the Bombard learned his view of the atonement from Abaelard and often had Abaelard’s Theologia in his hands, Migne, 199. 1052. See Denifle, pp. 616 sqq. Baltzer, pp. 96 sqq., goes so far as to say that his silence is to be interpreted as a denial of the Anselmic theory.

(100; original 1411): IV. 3; Migne, p. 335.

(101; original 1412): Quaedam summa res est Pater et Filius et Spiritus et illa non est generans neque genita nec procedens.

(102; original 1413): From time to time questionable articles continued to be cited from the Lombard. In the middle of the thirteenth century the number of such articles at variance with the doctrine of the Church was given as eight. The doctors of Paris increased the number. Eymeric wrote a treatise on twenty-two such heretical statements. A list of fifteen are given at the close of Peter’s Sentences. Migne, 451-454.

(103; original 1414): He is probably a different man from Alanus, archbishop of Auxerre, with whom he has often been identified, and who spent the last twenty years of his life at Clairvaux and wrote a life of St. Bernard. Migne, 186. 470-523. See Deutsch, Alanus, Herzog, I. 283 sqq. Hergenröther-Kirsch frequently quotes Alanus.

(104; original 1415): Regulae de sacra theologia, Migne, 210. 621-684; and de arte sive de articulis catholicae fidei, Migne, 593-617.

(105; original 1416): Congregatio fidelium confitentium Christum, et sacramentorum subsidium, Migne, p. 613. Under the title liber sententiarum, Migne, 229-264, he wrote also on the Lord’s birth, John the Baptist, and Mary.

(106; original 1417): Walter speaks of the four labyrinths as "treating with scholastic levity the mysteries of the Trinity and the incarnation and vomiting out many heresies." Planck gave an analysis of Walter’s work in Studien und Kritiken, 1844, pp. 823 sqq. Bulaeus, in Hist. universitatum, vol. II. 402, 629, gives extracts, which are reprinted in Migne, 199, pp. 1127 sqq. Denifle also gives quotations, Archiv, etc., 1886, pp. 404 sqq.

(107; original 1418): Harnack, Dogmengesch., III. 314 sqq., 373 sqq., turns to ridicule the alleged difference between scholasticism and mysticism. With the emotional or quietistic type of religion, die Pektoraltheologie, the cardiac theology, as the Germans call it, he has little sympathy. Piety, he says, is the starting-point of both and full knowledge their goal. He makes the brusque statement, p. 318, that "a mystic who does not become a Roman Catholic, is a dilettante." Ritschl had said before that there is "no normal mysticism except in connection with the hermit life. The love for it, widely prevalent among evangelical Christians, is dilettanteism."Pietismus, II. 12. Harnack, however, is willing to allow a distinction in the terms and to speak of scholasticism when the relation of God to the universe is thought of and of mysticism when we have in mind the union of the soul with God.

(108; original 1419): Paradiso, XXXI. 130, XXXIII. 49, etc. Dr. Philip Schaff said, Lit. and Poetry, p. 232, "Bernard defended orthodox mysticism and the theology of the heart against speculative rationalism and the theology of the intellect in contrast with Peter Abaelard."

(109; original 1420): "The mediaeval mystics were steeped in Dionysius." Inge, p. 110.

(110; original 1421): De perf. monachi, VIII.; Migne, 145. 303.

(111; original 1422): De preparat. ad contemplationem sive Benjamin minor, I. 73; Migne, 196. 52.

(112; original 1423): C. Faus. Man., XXII. 52.

(113; original 1424): Sermo in Cant., 51, 2. See De consid., I, 1.

(114; original 1425): Dogmengesch., III. 301, 305. For Bernard’s acquaintance with Scripture, see Ries, pp. 11 sq.

(115; original 1426): Ries, pp. 9, 15.

(116; original 1427): Omnia vestra in caritate fiant, Ep., 221. Melius est ut unus pereat quam unitas, Ep., 102; Migne, 182. 257.

(117; original 1428): Non ea disputatio comprehendit sed sanctitas, quoting Eph., III., 18. Sancti comprehendunt. De consid., V. 14; Migne, 182. 804.

(118; original 1429): Ep., 142, 2; Migne, 182, 297. Dr. Philip Schaff said that "love and humility were the crowning traits of Bernard’s character."Lit. and Poetry, p. 232.

(119; original 1430): Ries, pp. 35, sqq.

(120; original 1431): Causa diligendi Deum Deus est, modus sine modo diligere. De dilig. Deo. 1. Migne, 182. 974.

(121; original 1432): In Cant., p. 919, as quoted by Ries, p. 212.

(122; original 1433): Sic affici deificari est. Bernard does not shrink from the use of this word as also Origen and Gregory of Nyssa did not, and other Fathers who used it or its Greek equivalent.

(123; original 1434): Serm., LXXV. 2; LXIII. 1; LXXIII. 1, 2.

(124; original 1435): Serm., VIII. Migne, p. 810.

(125; original 1436): Divinitus inspiratus Christi et ecclesiae laudes, et sacri amoris gratiam et aeterni connubii cecinit sacramenta, etc. Serm., I. 8.; Migne, p. 788.

(126; original 1437): Serm., LXXIX. 1; Migne, p. 1163.

(127; original 1438): Haec mea philosophia scire Jesum Christum et hunc crucifixum. Serm., XLIII. 4; Migne, p. 995.

(128; original 1439): Jesus mel in ore, in aure melos, in corde jubilus. Serm., XV. 6; Migne, p. 847.

(129; original 1440): See Vacandard, Vie de S. Bernard, II. 497, and Ries, pp. 198 sq.

(130; original 1441): Unitas quam facit non confusio naturarum, sed voluntatum consensio. Serm. in Cant., LXXI. 7; Migne, 183. 1124. Harnack, whose treatment of St. Bernard is one of the most stirring chapters in his Hist. of Doctrine, nevertheless says unjustly III. 304, that Bernard’s mysticism naturally led to Pantheism. In Bernard himself there is no trace of Pantheism. See Ries, pp. 190 sq.

(131; original 1442): St. Victor, the convent which William of Champeaux, Hugo, and Richard made famous, had its filial houses not only in France but also in Ireland. With the French Revolution the convent and its grounds disappeared. Two streets of Paris, the Rue Guy de la Brosse and the Rue de Jussieu, were driven through them. See Wetzer-Welte, St. Victor, XII. 914 sqq.

(132; original 1443): The argument in favor of Saxony is well stated by Preger, Deutsche Mystik, I. 227 sqq. So Zöckler in Herzog, and the art. on Hugo, in Wetzer-Welte.

(133; original 1444): Summa Sententiarum, Migne, 176. 42-172. This work has been denied to Hugo by Denifle on insufficient grounds. Hugo opens the work with a treatment of the three cardinal virtues, faith, hope, and love, and proceeds to the discussion of the Trinity, creation, the five sacraments, and marriage.

(134; original 1445): He discusses the senses of Scripture, the number of the books, the apocrypha, the translation, the historical difficulties of Scripture, etc. See Migne, 175. 9-28. The same topics are treated in his treatise on Learning. Migne, 176. 778-811.

(135; original 1446): Among his mysticalwritings are de arca Noe morali, Migne, 176. 619-680; de arca mystica, Migne, 176. 681-703; de vanitate mundi. Noah’s ark is symbolical of the spiritual house and Christ is the "Captain, the supreme Noah." The wood, windows, and other parts of the ark are all spiritualized. In the second treatise the ark represents the cross.

(136; original 1447): Carnale cor quasi lignum viride necdum ab humore carnalis concupiscentiae exsiccatum, etc. See Liebner, p. 315.

(137; original 1448): De arca morali, III. 7; Migne, 176. 654.

(138; original 1449): de erud. didasc., Migne, 176. 797.

(139; original 1450): Quia non potest dei beatitudo participari nisi per intellectum, etc. Summa, II. 1: Migne, 176. 79.

(140; original 1451): See Liebner, Hugo von St. Victor, pp. 81 sq.

(141; original 1452): Migne, 196. 409.

(142; original 1453): .De Emmanuele, Migne, 196. 601-665

(143; original 1454): Migne, 196. 995-1011. Richard calls Bernard, divus Bernardus, and "my Bernard," V; Migne, 195. 999. He also addressed other works to St. Bernard.

(144; original 1455): O felix culpa quae talem ac tantum meruit habere redemptorem, Migne,196. 1003.

(145; original 1456): See Engelhardt, pp. 14 sqq.

(146; original 1457): Fides totius boni initium est atque fundamentum, Migne, 196. 889.

(147; original 1458): Interficit matrem ubi omnem supergreditur rationem. De prep., 86; Migne, 196. 62, etc.

(148; original 1459): Animus qui ad scientae altitudinem nititur ascendere, primum et principale sit ei studium se ipsum cognoscere. De prep., 76; Migne, 196. 54.

(149; original 1460): Contemplatio est libera mentis perspicacia in sapientae spectacula cum admiratione suspensa. De gratia, I. 5; Migne, 196. 67. Here, as in other places, Richard quotes his teacher Hugo.

(150; original 1461): Supra rationem et praeter rationem. De prep., 86; Migne, 196. 61.

(151; original 1462): De prep., 74; Migne, p. 54.

(152; original 1463): Suspecta mihi est omnis veritas, quam non confirmat scripturarum auctoritas. De prep., 81; Migne, 196. 57.

(153; original 1464): A fall edition of his works is given by Migne, vols. 167-170. See Bach and Schwane. Also Rocholl, Rupert von Deutz. Beitrag zur Gesch. der Kirche im 12ten Jahrh., Gütersloh, 1886.

(154; original 1465): Rupert gives an account of his journey to France to meet William and Anselm in disputation in his De regula Benedicti, I. 1; Migne, 170. 482 sq.

(155; original 1466): The name of the work is De operibus sanctae trinitatis Migne, 167. 199-1827. The first two parts represent the work of the Father and the Son and the third the work of the Holy Spirit, pp. 1571-1827.

(156; original 1467): Migne, 168. 841.

(157; original 1468): De operibus S. trinitatis, II. 10. Bellarmin pronounced Rupert a heretic because of his views on the Lord’s Supper. Schwane, Dogmengesch., p. 641, denies the charge.

脚注

    1. 米尔曼 (Milman) 在《拉丁基督教史》(Hist. of Lat. Christianity)，VIII. 257，说：“经院学者虽竭力探究那不可测度的，却没有测透任何事；虽拥有庞大的逻辑工具，却没有证明任何足以使探究之心满意的事。”这番话显然不公允。只须想到安瑟伦 (Anselm) 的本体论论证、托马斯·阿奎那 (Thomas Aquinas) 的宇宙论论证，以及他们就基督的补赎所作的精密阐述，便会觉得这一论断并不真实。
    ↩︎

  2. 见罗杰·培根 (Roger Bacon)：《大著作》(Opus Majus)，布里奇斯 (Bridges) 编，I. 54–56；桑迪斯 (Sandys)，《古典学术史》(Class. Scholarship)，第507、540–546、568及后页；以及塞思 (Seth)，Enc. Brit.，XXI. 419。↩︎

  3. 1209年的巴黎会议禁止使用他的《自然哲学》(Natural Philosophy)。格列高利九世 (Gregory IX) 于1231年谴责《物理学》(Physics)，但1254年巴黎大学却规定了讲解亚里士多德著作所须投入的课时数。↩︎

  4. Omnis hic excluditur, omnis est abjectus.

    Qui non Aristotelis venit armis tectus. “凡未披戴亚里士多德武装而来的人，在这里一概被排斥，一概被摈弃。”Chart.，I. 第xviii页。↩︎

  5. 库赞 (Cousin) 将其分为三个时期：第一时期，哲学从属于神学；第二时期，两者联合；第三时期，两者分离。↩︎

  6. “他异性” (Otherness)，圣维克多的理查 (Rich. de St. Victor) 用以指三位一体中的位格区别。↩︎

  7. 《论圣礼》(de sacram.)，I. 7；米涅版，176，290。↩︎

  8. 佩尔捷 (Peltier) 编本，V. 38。↩︎

  9. 托马斯·富勒 (Thomas Fuller) 风趣地将经院学者比作那些在伦敦小块土地上建房的人，“以高耸的思辨来扩展其狭小的根基”。↩︎

  10. H. 德尔根斯 (H. Doergens)，《共相学说》(Lehre von d. Universalien)，海德堡 (Heidelb.)，1867年；J. H. 勒韦 (J. H. Löwe)，《中世纪实在论与唯名论之争》(D. Kampf zwischen d. Realismus und Nominalismus im Mittelalter)，布拉格 (Prag)，1876年。韦策尔—韦尔特 (Wetzer-Welte)，XII. 305及后页，“共相” (Universalien) 条。另见各部哲学史。↩︎

  11. 据索尔兹伯里的约翰 (John of Salisbury) 说，这个问题上的不同见解不下十三种。见普兰特尔 (Prantl)，《逻辑史》(Gesch. der Logik)，II. 118。↩︎

  12. 波菲利 (Porphyry) 的这段话如下：mox de generibus et speciebus illud quidem sive subsistant, sive in solis nudis intellectibus posita sint, sive subsistentia corporalia sint an incorporalia, et utrum separata a sensibilibus, an insensibilibus posita et circa haec consistentia, dicere recusabo. Altissimum enim negotium est hujusmodi et majoris egens inquisitionis。“关于属和种，我暂不论述它们究竟是实存的，还是仅存在于纯粹的概念之中；若是实存的，究竟是有形的还是无形的；又究竟与感性事物分离，还是存在于非感性事物之中并依存于这些事物。因为这类问题极其深奥，需要更深入的探究。”见吉泽勒 (Gieseler)，《教会史》(Ch. Hist.)，德文版，III. 384。↩︎

  13. 弗赖辛的奥托 (Otto of Freising) 在《腓特烈事迹》(de gest. Frid.)，I. 47，称他为那个时代唯名论的创始者：qui primus nostris temporibus in logica sententiam vocum instituit，“他在我们这个时代首先在逻辑学中确立了语词学说”。据索尔兹伯里的约翰说，唯名论几乎随着罗瑟林 (Roscellinus) 而完全消失，见《逻辑学辩护》(Metalog.)，II. 17。↩︎

  14. Pseudo-Dialecticus，即“伪辩证家”。《书信》(Ep.)，21。《论三位一体的信仰》(De fide trin.)，3。tres personae sunt tres res sicut tres angeli aut tres animae, ita tamen ut voluntas et potestas omnino sunt idem，“三个位格是三个实在，如同三个天使或三个灵魂，然而其意志与能力却完全相同”。另见《书信》(Ep.)，II. 41。↩︎

  15. 洛夫斯 (Loofs)，第271页，说：“他也许是所有中世纪神学家中最重要的一位。”↩︎

  16. 丘奇 (Church) 生动地描绘了“在阿尔卑斯山涧声中安睡的荒野奥斯塔 (Aosta)”。奥斯塔原是罗马人的聚居地，名为奥古斯塔·普雷托里亚 (Augusta Praetoria)，约在五世纪成为主教驻地。↩︎

  17. 他的观点阐述于《论圣灵的发出》(de processione Spiritus Sancti)。他论证说，圣灵从父而出，并非因父是父，而是因父是上帝。因此，圣灵也必从身为上帝的子而出。↩︎

  18. 引文见弗里曼 (Freeman)，《威廉·鲁弗斯》(W. Rufus)，II. 661。↩︎

  19. 《天堂篇》(Paradiso)，XII. 137。↩︎

  20. 弗里曼在其《威廉·鲁弗斯》(W. Rufus)，II. 570–588，附有一篇论安瑟伦书信的专论。↩︎

  21. Qui non crediderit, non experietur, et qui expertus non fuerit non intelliget，“不信的人就不会经历，没有经历的人就不会理解。”《论三位一体的信仰》(de fide trin.)，2；米涅，158，264。↩︎

  22. 《书信》(Ep.)，II. 41；米涅，158. 1193。Christianus per fidem debet ad intellectum proficere non per intellectum ad fidem，“基督徒应当借着信仰进至理解，而不是借着理解进至信仰。”↩︎

  23. 《上帝为何成为人》(Cur Deus homo)，I. 2；米涅，158. 364。↩︎

  24. 埃德默 (Eadmer)：nihil asserere nisi quod aut canonicis aut Augustini dictis posse defendi videret，“凡他看不出能以正典或奥古斯丁的言论加以辩护的，他一概不作断言。”↩︎

  25. aliquid quo majus nihil cogitari potest，“一个不能设想有任何东西比它更伟大的存在”。↩︎

  26. si vel in solo intellectu est, potest cogitari esse et in re, quod majus est，“即使它只存在于理解之中，也可以设想它在现实中存在，而后者更为伟大。”↩︎

  27. 托马斯·阿奎那说，即使上帝之名意指 illud quo majus cogitari non potest，即“不能设想有任何东西比之更伟大的存在”，也不能由此进而肯定上帝的真实存在，因为无神论者否认有 aliquid quo majus cogitari non potest，即“不能设想有任何东西比之更伟大的存在”。《神学大全》(Summa)，I. ii. 2。黑格尔 (Hegel) 回答康德 (Kant) 说，the Begriff an und für sich selbst enthält das Sein also eine Bestimmtheit，即“自在自为的概念本身包含存在，作为一种规定性”。E. 凯尔德 (E. Caird) 教授在〈安瑟伦关于上帝存在的论证〉一文（《神学研究杂志》[Journal of Theolog. Studies]，1900年，第23–39页）中，总结他对安瑟伦论证的异议说：“这是对最初见于柏拉图哲学的一种观念所作的经院式歪曲”，等等。里奇尔 (Ritschl) 在《称义与和好》(Justification and Reconciliation)，第217页，提出同样的异议，说安瑟伦混淆了现实与思想。↩︎

  28. intelligere，即“理解”，与 in intellectu esse，即“存在于理解之中”。↩︎

  29. 克斯特林 (Köstlin) 在〈上帝存在的证明〉(D. Beweise fürs Dasein Gottes)，载于《研究与批评》(Studien u. Kritiken)，1875、1876年，对本体论论证的历史作了细致的阐述。另见鲁策 (Ruze)，《安瑟伦以来的上帝存在本体论证明》(D. ontol. Gottesbeweis seit Anselm)，哈勒 (Halle)，1882年。↩︎

  30. Quamvis homo juste a diabolo torqueretur, ipse tamen illum injuste torquebat, “虽然人受魔鬼折磨是公正的，魔鬼折磨人却是不公正的”，等等，I. 7；米涅，158. 367及下页。安瑟伦在II. 20，第427及下页，再次讨论这一点，说上帝为了解救人，并不需要降临征服魔鬼，也不需要对他采取司法程序。上帝对魔鬼所欠的，除了惩罚别无其他；人对魔鬼所欠的，也不过是以其人之道还治其人之身，即像人自己被征服那样去征服他。魔鬼所索取的一切，人都是欠上帝的，而不是欠魔鬼的。↩︎

  31. Non aliud est peccare quam Deo non reddere debitum，“犯罪无非是不向上帝偿还所欠的。”I. 11；米涅，第376页。↩︎

  32. pro contumelia illata plus reddere quam abstulit .... Debet omnis qui peccat, honorem quem rapuit, Deo solvere et haec est satisfactio quam omnis peccator Deo debet facere，“对于所加的侮辱，所偿还的必须多于所夺去的……凡犯罪的人，都应当将自己所夺去的尊荣偿还上帝，这就是每个罪人都应当向上帝作出的补赎。”↩︎

  33. Satisfactio quam nec potest facere nisi Deus nec debet nisi homo, necesse est ut eam faciat Deus-homo，“这补赎惟有上帝才能作出，却又惟有人才有义务作出，因此必须由神人来作出。”II. 6；米涅，第404页。↩︎

    1. 22；米涅，158. 431。安瑟伦在多大程度上借用了教父传下的补赎教义，或日耳曼法律中带有 Wehrgeld（偿命金）即荣誉之债的观念，乃至他究竟是否借用过这些，都是有争议的问题。教会的补赎制度很可能影响了骑士的荣誉观。哈纳克 (Harnack)，《教义史》(Dogmengesch.)，III. 252及下页，以及里奇尔，《称义》等，第263页，反对安瑟伦的论证，认为它建立在一种“暗示上帝与人在私权上平等的上帝公义观”之上。
    ↩︎

  35. 哈纳克对安瑟伦的论证给予了长篇的关注（《教义史》[Dogmengesch.]，III. 341–358），在逐项列出其优缺点时，断言其缺点远远超过优点。安瑟伦的理论完全不可接受，die Theorie ist völlig unannehmbar。哈纳克说，若非当今神学深陷传统主义，忽视福音、伦理、逻辑和文化的一切准则，本来无须对这些缺点多费唇舌。他宣称，上帝不能出于纯粹的爱而赦免，却必须借献祭使自己的尊荣得到满足，这是一种可怕的思想。单就安瑟伦的论证本身而言，它并不足以招致如此严厉的批评；若将他的其他著作及其本人的品格也考虑在内，他便可免于这些隐含的指控。↩︎

  36. 《默想或祷告》(Meditationes seu Orationes)，米涅，158. 709–1014。见哈塞 (Hasse)，I. 176–232。↩︎

  37. Jesu bone, quam dulcis es in corde cogitantis de te et diligentis te，“良善的耶稣啊，你在默想你、爱你之人的心中何等甘甜。”米涅，158. 770。↩︎

  38. 鲁尔 (Rule)，I. 48，根据亲眼观察，描写了横跨奥斯塔附近一条急流的古老桥梁——埃尔桥 (le Pont de l’Aël)；这座桥令人目眩。他说，安瑟伦作这番描述时，心中可能想到的就是它。↩︎

  39. Sine mensura profundum, et tenebrosa caligine horribiliter obscurum，“深不可测，被幽暗的浓雾笼罩，黑暗得令人恐惧。”米涅，158，719。↩︎

    ，69
    ，326。↩︎

  41. 他将自己的不幸追溯到此时以及威廉 (William) 的敌意。Hinc calamitatum mearum quae nunc perseverant coeperunt exordia et quo amplius fama extendebatur nostra, aliena in me succensa est invidia，“我那延续至今的灾难由此开始；我的名声越是远播，别人对我的嫉妒就越是炽烈。”米涅，第116页。↩︎

  42. Verborum usum habebat mirabilem, sed sensu contemptibilem et ratione vacuum，“他运用言辞令人惊叹，内容却鄙陋可轻，毫无理性。”米涅，第123页。↩︎

  46. 见他的描述，I. 47及后页。↩︎

  47. 阿伯拉尔 (Abaelard) 写给儿子的一封信保存了下来，流露出他的慈爱。父亲敦促儿子研读圣经。1150年的记载提到一位阿斯特拉拉比乌斯 (Astralabius)，属于南特 (Nantes) 的座堂参事会。豪斯拉特 (Hausrath)，第173页，推测他就是阿伯拉尔的儿子。↩︎

  48. Ut amplius mitagarem, obtuli me ei satisfacere eam scilicet quam corruperam mihi matrimonio copulando, dummodo id secreto fieret, ne famae detrimentum incurrerem。“为进一步平息他的怒气，我提出愿意补偿他，就是与我所玷污的女子结婚，但须秘密进行，免得我的名声受损。”米涅，第130页。↩︎

  49. Concupiscentia te mihi potius quam amicitia sociavit, libidinis ardor potius quam amor. “使你与我结合的，与其说是友爱，不如说是情欲；与其说是爱情，不如说是欲火。”《书信》(Ep.)，II.；米涅，第186页。↩︎

  50. 多伊奇 (Deutsch)，第35页。So war Abaelard Mönch geworden, nicht von innerem Verlangen getrieben，“阿伯拉尔就这样成了修士，并非受内心渴望的驱使”，等等。他与爱洛伊丝 (Heloise) 的关系，使他一时无法以公开教师的身份自由地在公众中活动。↩︎

  51. 《神学导论》(Introductio in theologiam)。关于这次审判，阿伯拉尔本人是我们的主要史料来源。见《苦难史》(Hist. Calam.)，米涅，第141–150页。另见弗赖辛的奥托。↩︎

  52. 阿伯拉尔在自传结尾宣称，自己如同另一个该隐，被拖着在地上流离飘荡；但他也引用论及上帝护理的经文，如万事都互相效力，叫爱上帝的人得益处。↩︎

  53. 《伯纳德书信》(Ep. Bernardi)，326；米涅，132. 531及后页。威廉将阿伯拉尔的《神学》(Theologia) 和其他著作寄给伯纳德，以证实自己的指控。他担心阿伯拉尔会成为一条无人能够消灭的“巨龙”。库特尔 (Kutter) 在《圣蒂埃里的威廉》(Wilhelm von St. Thierry)，第34、36、43、48页，反对多伊奇的看法，坚持认为威廉才是不久后阿伯拉尔受审一事的煽动者和发起人；伯纳德宁愿沉默安宁，不愿争斗，是威廉的呼吁才促使他采取行动。↩︎

  54. 《阿伯拉尔书信》(Ep. Abael.)，X.；米涅，178. 335。↩︎

  55. 伯纳德的传记作者高弗里德 (Gaufrid) 说，阿伯拉尔曾答应改正。在桑斯 (Sens) 的控告中，却未提及这一承诺；如果确曾作出这样的承诺，这种遗漏便难以理解。见雷米萨 (Remusat)，I. 172；以及普尔 (Poole)，第163页。↩︎

  56. 《伯纳德书信》(Ep. Bernardi)，189；米涅，182. 355。伯纳德描述了这次会面，并阐明阿伯拉尔的影响所带来的危险，见《书信》(Epp.)，187–194、330–338。关于这次审判，见拙文《作为教会人士的圣伯纳德》("St. Bernard the Churchman")，载《普林斯顿评论》(Princeton Rev.)，1903年，第180页及后。↩︎

  57. 这次预备会议的说法，依据的是贝伦加尔 (Berengar) 的证言，以及索尔兹伯里的约翰 (John of Salisbury)《教宗史》(Hist. Pontif.) 第VIII章9中的一段话。约翰在描述普瓦捷的吉尔伯特 (Gilbert of Poictiers) 受审时说，伯纳德想在一次预备会议上预先裁定吉尔伯特的案件，采用的正是他对付阿伯拉尔时所用的方法——arte sim ili magistrum Petrum agressus erat，即他曾以类似手法攻击彼得大师。贝伦加尔为阿伯拉尔所作的辩护，竟流于激烈的谩骂。见米涅，178. 1858及后页。贝伦加尔声称，在这次预备会议上，主教们和伯纳德酒酣耳热，作出了不利于阿伯拉尔的裁决。他叙述的细节及其对伯纳德的指控，与我们从其他方面所知的伯纳德品格完全不符。多伊奇 (Deutsch) 不像瓦康达尔 (Vacandard) 那样，能够为伯纳德在这次会议中所起的作用洗脱不公之嫌；见尼安德《圣伯纳德》(St. Bernard)，II. 1及后页。↩︎

  58. 这一说法与弗赖辛的奥托 (Otto of Freising) 的叙述并不矛盾。奥托是一位不偏不倚的记述者；据他说，阿伯拉尔拒绝申辩，是因为害怕引起民众骚乱。↩︎

  59. 米涅，182. 1049–1051。另见赫费勒 (Hefele)，V. 463及后页。↩︎

  60. 《书信》(Ep.)，331；米涅，182. 537。这类致枢机主教的书信共有九封，即188、192、193、331–335、338。最长的一封是致教宗的第190封；米涅，182. 1051–1071。这些书信措辞极其激烈，致使伯纳德在某些方面受到毫不留情的谴责。从基督徒应有的节制与仁爱来看，这些信令人难以理解，也无法为之辩护。豪斯拉特 (Hausrath)，第248页等处，将他描绘为“精于世故的克莱尔沃修道院院长”——der werltkluge Abt von Clairvaux，为了取得对阿伯拉尔不利的裁决而使尽外交手段。麦凯布 (M’Cabe) 在一章颇为引人入胜的论述中，第322–354页，也持同样看法。我们虽不为他开脱，但在作出判断时，必须记得，那个时代的人视异端为可怕之事。无疑，伯纳德也厌避阿伯拉尔，认为他追求的是喝彩，而非教会的进展。莫里森 (Morison)，第302页，说伯纳德意识到新时代的危险时，“有一种深沉黑暗的恐怖临到他身上”。尼安德 (Neander) 在《圣伯纳德》，II. 3中说，无人能够怀疑，伯纳德的热忱出于纯正的基督徒宗旨，但他却在圣洁之爱的面具下使用仇恨的武器。↩︎

  61. 米涅，178. 103。↩︎

  62. 《苦难史》(Story of Misfortunes) 写于他任圣吉尔达斯 (St. Gildas) 修道院院长期间。有人将它与奥古斯丁的《忏悔录》相比。然而，没有什么比较比这更令人遗憾地违背事实了。阿伯拉尔揭露自己的内心状态，是为了达到世俗的目的。他想引人注意自己，为新的生涯铺路。他写给爱洛伊丝 (Heloise) 的信，与其说是要使她相信自己的正统，不如说是要给教会当局留下这样的印象。这是多伊奇，第43页及后；豪斯拉特，第275页及后；以及尼奇 (Nitsch) 在赫尔佐格百科全书所撰“阿伯拉尔”(Abaelard) 条中所持的立场。↩︎

  63. 法国作者将阿伯拉尔的理论称为概念论 (Conceptualism)，认为他以 conceptus（概念）取代了 voces（语词）。多伊奇，第105页。十二世纪后半叶的作者沃尔特·马普 (Walter Map) 称阿伯拉尔为“唯名论者的领袖，princeps nominalismi；他在辩证法中所犯的错误，比他在处理圣经时所犯的还多”。赖特 (Wright) 编本，I. 24，第41页。↩︎

  64. 另见《神学导论》(Introd. ad Theol.)，米涅，178. 980。↩︎

  65. Dubitando ad inquisitionem venimus, inquirendo veritatem percipimus. “我们由怀疑而进入探究，由探究而认识真理。”《是与否》(Sic et Non)，米涅，第1349页。多伊奇，第159页及下页，称这种自由探究的精神——Die Freiheit der Forschung——为贯穿阿伯拉尔全部著作的基调。↩︎

  66. 《苦难史》(Hist. Calam.)，米涅，178. 142。Nec credi posse aliquid nisi primitus intellectus，即任何事物若未经首先理解，便不可能相信，等等。↩︎

  67. 《神学导论》(Introd. ad Theol.)，米涅，第1051页，另见第959页。Fides quippe dicitur existimatio non apparentium, cognitio vero ipsarum rerum experientia per ipsam earam praesentiam。即所谓信仰，是对未显现之事的判断；而知识，则是借着事物本身的呈现而对它们有所体验。↩︎

  68. Credimus ut cognoscamus; nisi credideritis, non intelligetis。“我们信，为要认识；你们若不信，就不能明白。”其他引文见赫费勒，V. 463–469；另见多伊奇论“信仰与知识”的一章，第168页及后。↩︎

  69. 伯纳德、桑斯宗教会议及弗赖辛的奥托所提的指控，皆是如此。见《腓特烈事迹》(De gestis Frid.)，48。↩︎

  70. 《神学导论》(Introd. ad Theol.)，米涅，第986页。↩︎

  71. 《神学导论》(Introd. ad Theol.)，米涅，第1052页。↩︎

  72. Catholica quippe est fides, id est universalis quae ita omnibus necessaria est ut nemo discretus absque ea salvari possit, 即“信仰是大公的，也就是普世的；它对所有人都是如此必需，以致凡有辨识能力的人，没有它便不能得救”。米涅，第986页。鉴于这样的表述，普尔的评论颇有道理：“对他的控告之所以有力，实际上并不在于阿伯拉尔所得的结论，而在于他得出这些结论的方法。”第153页。↩︎

  73. 这些见于他的《罗马书注释》(Com. on Romans)，也见于《神学导论》(Introd. ad Theol.) 及《讲道集》(Sermons) 第V、X、XII篇。↩︎

  74. 这些观点在伦理学论著《认识你自己》(Scito te ipsum) 和《罗马书注释》(Com. on Romans) 中有更详尽的阐述，尤其见附于第V章之后的一篇论原罪的专论，米涅，第866–874页。↩︎

  75. 他认为，那棵果实激起性欲的树是葡萄树。《六日创世论》(Hexameron)，米涅，第777页。↩︎

  76. 《罗马书注释》(Com. on Romans)，第II章6。多伊奇，第344页及后，详论了阿伯拉尔关于罪的看法。↩︎

  77. 《神学导论》(Introd. ad Theol.)，米涅，第1008页。↩︎

  78. 豪斯拉特，第293页及后，将阿伯拉尔列在这类殉难者的前列。他用以下的话为阿伯拉尔不肯坚持自己结论的做法辩护：“要求一个处在这种环境压力之下的学者（即中世纪教会主义的压力之下），具备农场雇工的勇气，或像政治家那样将自己的观点贯彻到合乎逻辑的结论，是不公平的。”↩︎

  79. 阿伯拉尔身后留下了一批仰慕他的学生，其中有些人，如奥姆尼贝内 (Omnibene)，以老师的《神学》(Theology) 为基础撰写《箴言集》(Sentences)，并沿用他将内容分为信仰、圣礼和爱三部分的方式。见德尼弗勒 (Denifle)，《文献汇刊》(Archiv)，第613页及后。↩︎

  80. 德尼弗勒将伦巴德 (the Lombard) 列入阿伯拉尔的神学学派。见其《阿伯拉尔的〈箴言集〉及其〈神学〉的改编本》(Abaelard’s Sentenzen und d. Bearbeitungen seiner Theologie)，《文献汇刊》(Archiv)，1885年，第613–624页。↩︎

  81. 尼安德—多伊奇 (Neander-Deutsch)，《圣伯纳德》(St. Bernard)，II. 131。普尔，第181页，称吉尔伯特对三位一体的阐释为“中世纪对神学形而上学所作的最精微、最详尽的贡献之一”。↩︎

  82. 《教宗史》(Hist. pontif.)，VIII；米涅，第522页及后。控告者之一是小桥的亚当 (Adam du Petit Pont)，一位英国人，后来任圣亚萨 (St. Asaph) 主教。他因在连接巴黎与拉丁区的一座小桥上设立学校而得此名。沙尔施密特 (Schaarschmidt)，第13页。↩︎

  83. 弗赖辛的奥托将四项具体指控列举如下：1. divina essentia non est deus，神的本质不是神。2. proprietates personarum non sunt ipsae personae，位格的特性不是位格本身。3. theolog. personae in nulla praedicantur propositione，神学中的位格在任何命题中都不作谓词。4. dimna natura non est incarnata，神性并未道成肉身。高弗里德 (Gaufried) 对前三项的表述略有不同，见米涅，185. 617。↩︎

  84. 斯蒂芬斯 (Stephens) 称他为“他那个世纪最杰出的英国学者，远非他人可比”。《英国教会史》(Hist. of the Engl. Ch.)，第320页及后。↩︎

  85. 沙尔施密特称此书为“中世纪文献中第一部伟大的国家理论著作”。鉴于其内容的多样，普尔，第218页，说：“它在某种程度上是一部十二世纪中叶知识界思想的百科全书。”↩︎

  86. 普尔说：“就古典文献阅读的广度和深度而言，他那个时代没有一位作者可与他并列。”《英国名人传记辞典》(Dict. of Natl. Biog.)，XXIX. 441。沙尔施密特也说，他对古典文献的熟悉令人惊叹——eine staunenswerthe Vertrautheit。↩︎

  87. 《逻辑学辩护》(Metalog.)，VII. 2。↩︎

  88. 这是巴黎一篇古老墓志铭所记的日期；但1160年已有一位巴黎主教获任为伦巴德的继任者，因此这一日期并不确定。这似乎表明他死于当时，除非他因买卖圣职的罪名而被罢免；圣维克多的沃尔特 (Walter of St. Victor) 说，他确实犯有此罪。米涅，199. 1140。↩︎

  89. Liber quem dicunt sententarium，“他们所谓的《箴言集》”，《书信》(Ep.)，188；米涅，182. 668。圣维克多的沃尔特宣称，此书或出自阿伯拉尔之手，或摘自他的著作——aut ex libris ejus excerptus。见多伊奇，《彼得·阿伯拉尔》附论 (P. Abaelard excursus)。↩︎

  90. 德尼弗勒在1885年的《文献汇刊》(Archiv) 中，以博学的考证确立了这些著作与阿伯拉尔的关系。它们的手稿现存于纽伦堡 (Nürnberg)、慕尼黑 (Munich) 等地。奥姆内贝内 (Omnebene) 明确声明，他的著作是取自不同来源的汇编。↩︎

  91. 《神学箴言集》(Sententiarum theologicarum libri)，VII；米涅，第186卷。他的姓名也拼作 Pullein、Pullan 等。见拉什多尔 (Rashdall) 在《英国名人传记辞典》(Dict. of Nat’l Biogr.) 所撰的条目，XLVII. 19。↩︎

  92. 耶稣会士波塞文 (Possevin) 列出了246部已刊行的注释。见韦策尔—韦尔特 (Wetzer-Welte)，IX. 1921，其中称注释的数量为 unzaelig，即“不可胜数”。赫根勒特 (Hergenröther) 在《历史》(Gesch.)，II. 516中，也同样用 zahllos（无数）形容它们。据韦尔纳 (Werner)《托马斯·阿奎那》(Thom. von Aquino)，I. 314，第一部注释出自塞涅莱的威廉 (William of Seignelay)；他曾在巴黎任教，后来成为巴黎主教。↩︎

  93. 《箴言集》序言 (Prolog. to the Sentences)：brevi volumine complicans patrum sententias appositis eorum eorum testimoniis，即“以简短的篇幅汇集教父们的论断，并附上他们的证言”，等等。↩︎

  94. 巴尔策 (Baltzer)，第2–5页，给出了细致研究的结果。其中引自奥古斯丁的有1000处；希拉里 (Hilary) 次之，被引用86次。巴尔策的著作煞费苦心地逐段比较伦巴德与教父们，以及他在经院学者中的前辈，尤其是阿伯拉尔和圣维克多的雨果 (Hugo of St. Victor)。↩︎

  95. 关于他也引用了甘杜尔夫 (Gandulf) 的《箴言集》(Sentences) 这一说法，其依据是德尼弗勒（《文献汇刊》[Archiv]，第621页及后）。该书手稿至今仍存于都灵 (Turin)。↩︎

  97. Reprobatio Dei est praescientia militiae in quibusdam non finiendae, et praeparatio poenae non terminandae。上帝的弃绝，是预知某些人永不止息的争战，并为他们预备永无止境的刑罚。↩︎

  99. Mors nos justificat, dum per eam caritas excitatur in cordibus nostris，“死使我们称义，因为爱借此在我们心中被激发起来”，III. 19；米涅，第285页。他的学生康沃尔的约翰 (John of Cornwall) 明确说，伦巴德从阿伯拉尔那里学得了他的赎罪观，并且时常手持阿伯拉尔的《神学》(Theologia)；米涅，199. 1052。见德尼弗勒，第616页及后。巴尔策，第96页及后，甚至说，应将他的沉默理解为对安瑟伦理论的否定。↩︎

  101. Quaedam summa res est Pater et Filius et Spiritus et illa non est generans neque genita nec procedens. “有某一至高的实在，就是父、子、灵；这一实在既非生者，亦非受生者，也非发出者。”↩︎

  102. 人们仍不时从伦巴德的著作中援引出可疑的条文。十三世纪中叶，据称这类与教会教义不符的条文有八项。巴黎的博士们又增加了这一数目。埃梅里克 (Eymeric) 撰文论述了二十二项这样的异端言论。彼得的《箴言集》(Sentences) 末尾列出了十五项。米涅，第451–454页。↩︎

  103. 他很可能并非欧塞尔 (Auxerre) 的阿拉努斯大主教 (Alanus)，尽管人们常将二者视为一人。后者在克莱尔沃 (Clairvaux) 度过了生命的最后二十年，并撰写过一部《圣伯纳德传》。米涅，186. 470–523。见多伊奇在赫尔佐格百科全书所撰“阿拉努斯”条，I. 283及后页。赫根勒特—基尔施 (Hergenröther-Kirsch) 经常引用阿拉努斯。↩︎

  104. 《神圣神学规则》(Regulae de sacra theologia)，米涅，210. 621–684；以及《关于大公信仰的技艺或条款》(de arte sive de articulis catholicae fidei)，米涅，第593–617页。↩︎

  105. Congregatio fidelium confitentium Christum, et sacramentorum subsidium，“承认基督的信徒的会众，以及圣礼的扶助”，米涅，第613页。他还以《箴言集》(liber sententiarum) 为题，论述主的降生、施洗约翰和马利亚，米涅，第229–264页。↩︎

  106. 沃尔特说，这四座迷宫“以经院式的轻浮态度处理三位一体和道成肉身的奥秘，吐出许多异端”。普朗克 (Planck) 在《研究与评论》(Studien und Kritiken)，1844年，第823页及后，对沃尔特的著作作了分析。布莱乌斯 (Bulaeus) 在《大学史》(Hist. universitatum)，第II卷，第402、629页，提供了摘录；这些摘录重印于米涅，199，第1127页及后。德尼弗勒也提供了引文，见《文献汇刊》(Archiv) 等，1886年，第404页及后。↩︎

  107. 哈纳克 (Harnack) 在《教义史》(Dogmengesch.)，III. 314及后页、373及后页，对经院主义与神秘主义之间所谓的区别加以嘲讽。对于情感型或寂静主义型的宗教，即德国人所谓的 die Pektoraltheologie（心灵神学），他少有同情。他说，敬虔是二者的起点，完满的知识是二者的目标。他在第318页直截了当地断言：“一个不成为罗马天主教徒的神秘主义者，不过是个业余爱好者。”里奇尔 (Ritschl) 此前曾说：“除与隐修生活相联系者外，并无正常的神秘主义。福音派基督徒中广为流行的对它的喜爱，不过是业余爱好。”《敬虔主义》(Pietismus)，II. 12。不过，哈纳克愿意容许在用语上有所区别：思想上帝与宇宙的关系时，可称为经院主义；想到灵魂与上帝的联合时，则称为神秘主义。↩︎

  108. 《天堂篇》(Paradiso)，XXXI. 130，XXXIII. 49等。菲利普·沙弗 (Philip Schaff) 博士在《文学与诗歌》(Lit. and Poetry)，第232页说：“伯纳德维护正统的神秘主义和心灵的神学，反对思辨的理性主义和理智的神学，与彼得·阿伯拉尔 (Peter Abaelard) 形成对照。”↩︎

  109. “中世纪的神秘主义者深受狄奥尼修斯 (Dionysius) 的熏陶。”英奇 (Inge)，第110页。↩︎

  110. 《论修士的完全》(De perf. monachi)，VIII；米涅 (Migne)，145. 303。↩︎

  111. 《论默观的预备，或小便雅悯》(De preparat. ad contemplationem sive Benjamin minor)，I. 73；米涅，196. 52。↩︎

  112. 《驳摩尼教徒浮士德》(C. Faus. Man.)，XXII. 52。↩︎

  113. 《雅歌讲道集》(Sermo in Cant.)，51，2。参见《论省察》(De consid.)，I，1。↩︎

  114. 《教义史》(Dogmengesch.)，III. 301、305。关于伯纳德对圣经的熟悉程度，见里斯 (Ries)，第11页及下页。↩︎

  115. 里斯 (Ries)，第9、15页。↩︎

  116. Omnia vestra in caritate fiant，“凡你们所做的，都要凭爱心而做”，《书信集》(Ep.)，221。Melius est ut unus pereat quam unitas，“宁可一人灭亡，也不可使合一毁灭”，《书信集》(Ep.)，102；米涅，182. 257。↩︎

  117. Non ea disputatio comprehendit sed sanctitas，“领悟这些的，不是辩论，而是圣洁”，引《以弗所书》(Eph.)，III. 18。Sancti comprehendunt，“圣徒能够领悟”。《论省察》(De consid.)，V. 14；米涅，182. 804。↩︎

  118. 《书信集》(Ep.)，142，2；米涅，182，297。菲利普·沙弗 (Philip Schaff) 博士说：“爱与谦卑是伯纳德品格中最卓越的特征。”《文学与诗歌》(Lit. and Poetry)，第232页。↩︎

  119. 里斯 (Ries)，第35页及后。↩︎

  120. Causa diligendi Deum Deus est, modus sine modo diligere，“爱上帝的原因就是上帝，爱他的尺度就是毫无限度地爱”。《论爱上帝》(De dilig. Deo)，1。米涅，182. 974。↩︎

  121. 《雅歌讲道集》(In Cant.)，第919页，转引自里斯 (Ries)，第212页。↩︎

  122. Sic affici deificari est，“有这样的情怀，就是被神化”。伯纳德并不避讳使用这个词，正如俄利根 (Origen) 和尼撒的贵格利 (Gregory of Nyssa)，以及其他使用此词或其希腊文对应词的教父，也不避讳一样。↩︎

  123. 《讲道集》(Serm.)，LXXV. 2；LXIII. 1；LXXIII. 1、2。↩︎

  124. 《讲道集》(Serm.)，VIII。米涅，第810页。↩︎

  125. Divinitus inspiratus Christi et ecclesiae laudes, et sacri amoris gratiam et aeterni connubii cecinit sacramenta，“他受神的默示，歌颂基督与教会，歌咏神圣之爱的恩典与永恒婚姻的奥秘”，等等。《讲道集》(Serm.)，I. 8；米涅，第788页。↩︎

  126. 《讲道集》(Serm.)，LXXIX. 1；米涅，第1163页。↩︎

  127. Haec mea philosophia scire Jesum Christum et hunc crucifixum，“我的哲学，就是知道耶稣基督并他钉十字架”。《讲道集》(Serm.)，XLIII. 4；米涅，第995页。↩︎

  128. Jesus mel in ore, in aure melos, in corde jubilus，“耶稣在口中是蜜，在耳中是乐曲，在心中是欢呼”。《讲道集》(Serm.)，XV. 6；米涅，第847页。↩︎

  129. 见瓦康达尔 (Vacandard)，《圣伯纳德传》(Vie de S. Bernard)，II. 497；以及里斯 (Ries)，第198页及下页。↩︎

  130. Unitas quam facit non confusio naturarum, sed voluntatum consensio，“造成这种合一的，不是本性的混淆，而是意志的一致”。《雅歌讲道集》(Serm. in Cant.)，LXXI. 7；米涅，183. 1124。哈纳克 (Harnack) 在其《教义史》中对圣伯纳德的论述，是全书最激动人心的篇章之一；然而，他在III. 304却不公正地说，伯纳德的神秘主义自然导向泛神论。伯纳德本人丝毫没有泛神论的痕迹。见里斯 (Ries)，第190页及下页。↩︎

  131. 圣维克多修道院 (St. Victor) 因尚波的威廉 (William of Champeaux)、休 (Hugo) 和理查 (Richard) 而闻名，其分院不仅遍及法国，也设在爱尔兰。法国大革命期间，修道院及其院地不复存在。巴黎的居伊·德·拉布罗斯街 (Rue Guy de la Brosse) 和朱西厄街 (Rue de Jussieu) 两条街道从其旧址穿过。见韦策尔—韦尔特 (Wetzer-Welte)，“圣维克多”(St. Victor) 条，XII. 914及后页。↩︎

  132. 普雷格尔 (Preger) 在《德国神秘主义》(Deutsche Mystik)，I. 227及后页，对支持萨克森 (Saxony) 之说的论据作了充分陈述。策克勒 (Zöckler) 在赫尔佐格 (Herzog) 百科全书中的论述，以及韦策尔—韦尔特 (Wetzer-Welte) 百科全书的“休”(Hugo) 条，也持此说。↩︎

  133. 《箴言总论》(Summa Sententiarum)，米涅，176. 42–172。德尼弗勒 (Denifle) 否认此书出自休 (Hugo)，但理由并不充分。休在书中首先论述信、望、爱这三种主要德行，继而讨论三位一体、创造、五种圣礼和婚姻。↩︎

  134. 他讨论了圣经的各种意义、书卷数目、次经、译本、圣经中的历史难题等。见米涅，175. 9–28。他在《论学习》一书中也讨论了同样的题目。米涅，176. 778–811。↩︎

  135. 他的神秘主义著作包括《挪亚方舟的道德意义》(de arca Noe morali)，米涅，176. 619–680；《方舟的奥秘意义》(de arca mystica)，米涅，176. 681–703；以及《论世界的虚空》(de vanitate mundi)。挪亚方舟象征属灵的殿宇，基督则是“船长，至高的挪亚”。方舟的木料、窗户和其他各部分，都被赋予属灵的意义。在第二部论著中，方舟代表十字架。↩︎

  136. Carnale cor quasi lignum viride necdum ab humore carnalis concupiscentiae exsiccatum，“属肉体的心如同青木，尚未脱尽肉体情欲的湿气”，等等。见利布纳 (Liebner)，第315页。↩︎

  137. 《方舟的道德意义》(De arca morali)，III. 7；米涅，176. 654。↩︎

  138. 《论学习》(de erud. didasc.)，米涅，176. 797。↩︎

  139. Quia non potest dei beatitudo participari nisi per intellectum，“因为唯有借着理智，才能分享上帝的福乐”，等等。《总论》(Summa)，II. 1；米涅，176. 79。↩︎

  140. 见利布纳 (Liebner)，《圣维克多的休》(Hugo von St. Victor)，第81页及下页。↩︎

  141. 米涅，196. 409。↩︎

  142. 《论以马内利》(De Emmanuele)，米涅，196. 601–665。↩︎

  143. 米涅，196. 995–1011。理查 (Richard) 称伯纳德为 divus Bernardus（神圣的伯纳德），又称他为“我的伯纳德”，V；米涅，195. 999。他还将其他著作献给圣伯纳德。↩︎

  144. O felix culpa quae talem ac tantum meruit habere redemptorem，“啊，有福的罪过，竟配得这样一位伟大的救赎主”，米涅，196. 1003。↩︎

  145. 见恩格尔哈特 (Engelhardt)，第14页及后。↩︎

  146. Fides totius boni initium est atque fundamentum，“信心是一切善的开端和根基”，米涅，196. 889。↩︎

  147. Interficit matrem ubi omnem supergreditur rationem，“当它超越一切理性时，便杀死了母亲”。《论预备》(De prep.)，86；米涅，196. 62等处。↩︎

  148. Animus qui ad scientae altitudinem nititur ascendere, primum et principale sit ei studium se ipsum cognoscere，“心灵若努力攀登知识的高峰，其首要而根本的功课，就当是认识自己”。《论预备》(De prep.)，76；米涅，196. 54。↩︎

  149. Contemplatio est libera mentis perspicacia in sapientae spectacula cum admiratione suspensa，“默观是心灵自由的洞察，怀着惊叹凝神于智慧的景象”。《论恩典》(De gratia)，I. 5；米涅，196. 67。在这里，如同在其他地方一样，理查引用了他的老师休的话。↩︎

  150. Supra rationem et praeter rationem，“超乎理性，也在理性之外”。《论预备》(De prep.)，86；米涅，196. 61。↩︎

  151. 《论预备》(De prep.)，74；米涅，第54页。↩︎

  152. Suspecta mihi est omnis veritas, quam non confirmat scripturarum auctoritas，“凡未经圣经权威证实的真理，我都存疑”。《论预备》(De prep.)，81；米涅，196. 57。↩︎

  153. 米涅第167–170卷收有其著作的完整版本。见巴赫 (Bach) 和施瓦内 (Schwane)。另见罗霍尔 (Rocholl)，《道依茨的鲁佩特：十二世纪教会史论稿》(Rupert von Deutz. Beitrag zur Gesch. der Kirche im 12ten Jahrh.)，居特斯洛 (Gütersloh)，1886年。↩︎

  154. 鲁佩特 (Rupert) 在《论本笃会规》(De regula Benedicti)，I. 1，叙述了他前往法国与威廉 (William) 和安瑟伦 (Anselm) 会面辩论的经过；米涅，170. 482及下页。↩︎

  155. 此书名为《论圣三一的作为》(De operibus sanctae trinitatis)，米涅，167. 199–1827。前两部分阐述圣父与圣子的作为，第三部分阐述圣灵的作为，第1571–1827页。↩︎

  156. 米涅，168. 841。↩︎

  157. 《论圣三一的作为》(De operibus S. trinitatis)，II. 10。贝拉明 (Bellarmin) 因鲁佩特关于圣餐的观点而判定他是异端。施瓦内 (Schwane) 在《教义史》(Dogmengesch.)，第641页，否认这一指控。↩︎
